Let's design a robust `spectral_iterations(a, T, iters, deadline)`:
- Normalize a to sum 1.
- For each iter:
   B = conv_full_fft(a)
   curJ = 2n max B.
   If curJ < best update.
   # Create target B:
   if T is None: target = B (no cap)
   else:
       target = np.minimum(B, T)
       # adjust sum to 1 while max≤T:
       s = target.sum()
       if s < 1:
          # add to capacity
          cap = T - target
          total_cap = cap.sum()
          if total_cap > 1e-15:
             add = (1 - s) / total_cap
             target += add * cap
          else:
             target = np.full_like(B, T)
       elif s > 1:
          target *= (1.0/s)
   # optional smoothing? target = gaussian? Maybe use exact.
   F = np.fft.fft(target)
   # square root with conjugate symmetry:
   A = np.empty_like(F, dtype=complex)
   A[0] = complex(np.sqrt(F[0].real), 0) # F[0]=1 real
   half=(M-1)//2
   for k in 1..half:
      ck = np.sqrt(F[k])
      A[k]=ck
      A[M-k]=np.conj(ck)
   # Due to branch, a_full may have imaginary if not exactly; take real.
   a_full = np.fft.ifft(A).real
   # Choose window shift:
   # Option 1: take first n; Option 2: best shift among several: 0, maybe argmax sum window, maybe fallback.
   # To avoid high cost, evaluate a few shifts.
   for s in shifts:
       cand = a_full[s:s+n]
       cand = np.maximum(cand, 0)
       S=cand.sum()
       if S < 1e-12 continue
       cand /= S  # sum 1
       # optional: if many zeros? ok
       # compute score; if improves or if last good, set a=cand
       # But for iteration, we need one a. Choose candidate with lowest score among shifts.
   a = best_cand
   # T anneal: if you have current score maybe T = min(T, bestJ/M) or scale.
- Return best.

But if projection yields negative and clipping too aggressive, maybe not improve. Could also use `a_new = a_full[s:s+n]` but allow negative small and fill with zeros? Maybe better to not clip too hard: `cand = a_full[s:s+n]; cand = np.where(cand > 0, cand, 0); cand /= cand.sum()`.

We can also consider using the "least squares projection" onto nonnegativity by adding a constant to eliminate negatives: `cand = a_full[s:s+n] - min(cand)` then normalize. This preserves shape better than zeroing. But nonnegative projection onto sum simplex of a vector v is not simply clipping; adding min(v) makes all nonnegative but increases lower values. If v has small negatives, good. We can do `cand = v - v.min()`; if many negative large, folds. Then normalize. Which is better? Clipping zeros large negative masses; adding min may create positive floor. We can try both: candidate1 clip, candidate2 shift. Evaluate and choose best score. Also maybe use rank-preserving? Try both, plus original a if no improve.

2. `def gradient_search(a0, budget_s, max_iter=10000):`
   - Normalize.
   - Track best.
   - Use a loop with time.
   - Every step compute b and score.
   - Choose weights: if step%? use top K. K schedule: start 1? A subgradient of max may get stuck in bad peak; use top K=32 or K=100. Softmax perhaps robust.
   - We can use multiple modes: alternate "hard" top 1, top 20, softmax tau=20, top 100. Stochastic descent. For each mode compute gradient. Then line search over lrs. Project to simplex.
   - Gradient can be computed with top_k slices for top 1. For top K, need weights on selected indices (not contiguous); using FFT correlation with weight vector length M sparse but okay. Build w zeros, set weights. For K small, direct sum over selected peaks: For each peak p, grad_j += 2 a[p-j] valid. Could do vectorized for K up to 128: for each p, slice. O(K n). Full-space optimization functions:
1. `_spectral_project(a, T, L, shift_candidates)`: one iteration returns best candidate and anc? We'll implement `_spectral_search(a0, deadline, iters_limit=None, T_end=None)`.
Need time budget and T schedule.
Pseudo:
```
def spectral_search(a0, deadline, t_start_score=None, t_target=None, max_iter=500):
    a = _normalize(a0.copy())
    n=len(a); M=2*n-1; L=_fast_len(M)
    b0=_conv_full(a); keepest...
    best=a; bestJ=_score(a)
    T = bestJ/(2*n) if t_start_score is None else t_start_score
    Tend = t_target/(2*n) if t_target else bestJ/(2*n*2?) 
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       b=_conv_full(a)
       J=2*n*max(b)
       if J<best: best=...
       if T is None: T=J/(2*n)
       # create target
       target=np.minimum(b, max(T, 1/M+1e-12))
       s=target.sum()
       if s < 1-1e-12:
          cap=max(T, 1/M+1e-12)-target
          tot=cap.sum()
          if tot>0: target += (1-s)*cap/tot
       elif s > 1+1e-12:
          target *= 1/s
       F=np.fft.fft(target, L)
       A=np.empty(L, complex)
       A[0]=complex(math.sqrt(max(0,F[0].real)),0)
       for k in range(1, L//2):
           ck=np.sqrt(F[k])
           A[k]=ck; A[L-k]=np.conj(ck)
       if L%2==0:
           # Nyquist
           val=F[L//2]
           A[L//2]=complex(math.sqrt(max(0,val.real)),0)  # if imag small
       a_full=np.fft.ifft(A).real
       # candidate shifts
       # find best s among 0, maybe windows with max positive sum
       shifts=[0]
       # argmax of window sum: simple search over multiples? We can approximate by cumulative sum of pos.
       pos=np.maximum(a_full,0)

```
def _band_objective(p, n, freqs):
    L = len(freqs); r = p[0]
    y = np.ones(n)
    for l,f in enumerate(freqs):
       c = p[1+2*l]; s = p[2+2*l]
       y += c * np.cos(2*np.pi*f*np.arange(n) + l?) + s * np.sin...
    y -= y.mean()
    y /= max(1, np.max(np.abs(y)))
    a = 1.0 + r*y
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    S=a.sum(); if S < 1e-12 return inf
    a /= S
    b=_conv_full(a); return 2*n*b.max()
```

Implement LP function:
```
def _lp_direction(f, M):
    n=len(f)
    offsets=[0]+list(range(1,n))+list(range(-1,-n,-1))? Need list from 1 to n-1 then -1..-(n-1)
    diags=[np.full(n,f[0])]
    for d in range(1,n):
       vals=np.full(n-d, f[d])
       diags.append(vals); diags.append(vals)
    offsets=[0]+[d for d in range(1,n)]+[-d for d in range(1,n)]
    A=scipy.sparse.diags(diags, offsets, shape=(2*n-1,n), format='csc')
    c=-np.ones(n)
    res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1,M), bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
```
But `scipy.sparse.diags` with offsets list and data list: Need order of data align. In example, offsets [0,1,...n-1,-1,...]. data lengths as above. For offset 1, data length n-1 places on row col+1 from top? Yes. For offset -1, data length n-1 places row col-1 from top? Yes.
Could be memory heavy for n=1024: constraints 2047, vars 1024, nnz ~2.1M, okay. `linprog` dense conversion maybe. For n=512, 0.5M.
LP M: Use sum=1, M = current peak * (1+1e-6) to ensure feasible f. Solve. If fail, return None.
Then `g=res.x`; normalize sum1. Score. Try blends t=1,0.8,0.5,0.2? If t=1 (g) may be good; if improvements. For each blend, `a_blend=(1-t)*f+t*g`; normalize; score; if <best accept. If accept, return a_blend; else try next M maybe 1.01.

          # continue with new M
          pass

```

def _band_optimize(n, freqs, x0, maxiter=150):

Let's define detailed functions:

`def _score_seq(a):`
```
a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0)
S=a.sum(); n=len(a)
if S<=0: return inf
b=_conv_full(a)
return float(2.0*n*b.max()/(S*S))
```
Could normalize to sum1 to avoid overflow? `_conv_full` works with original values. If parent values maybe large; objective scale invariant but FFT with large values okay. Normalize candidates to sum1 for all optimization. `_score_seq` can receive normalized. We'll have `_normalize` sum1.

`def _normalize(a, target=1.0):`
```
a=np.array(a,float); a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0, posinf=1000, neginf=0); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
S=a.sum()
if S<=1e-12: return np.full_like(a, target/len(a))
return a*(target/S)
```
Clipping to 1000 not needed during optimization (sum1 values small). For final, evaluator caps.

`def _conv_full(a):`
```
n=len(a); M=2*n-1
if n<128: return np.convolve(a,a)
L=_fast_len(M)
A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:M]
return b
```
Check: For even L, A*A complex; irfft returns real. Good. Ensure nonnegative small: `np.maximum(b,0)`? For objective max, negative small shouldn't matter; but target fill uses min. Could set `b[b<0 & >-1e-12]=0`. But if actual negatives due to FFT error? Conv nonnegative; tiny neg okay. Use `np.maximum(b,0.0)` perhaps affects gradient weights slightly but okay.

`def _score_from_b(a,b):`
```
n=len(a); S=a.sum(); return 2n*max(b)/S^2
```
Since we often have b. Use S=1 maybe. If normalized, score=2n*max(b). But after normalization S maybe 1. Use general.

`def _grad_peak(a,p):` direct.
```
grad=np.zeros(n)
lo=max(0,p-n+1); hi=min(n-1,p)
if hi>=lo:
  idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1); grad[idx]=2.0*a[p-idx]
return grad
```
For p integer. `a[p-idx]` if p-idx within [0,n-1]. Good.

`def _grad_top_direct(a, peaks, K):`
```
grad=zeros; for p in peaks: ...; grad/=K? If weights equal 1/K. We can multiply by 1/K.
```
If peaks include p values int.

`def _grad_weighted(a,w):` with FFT.
```
n=len(a); M=2*n-1; L=_fast_len(M+n-1)  # 3n-2
Fa=np.fft.fft(a,L); Fw=np.fft.fft(w,L)
r=np.fft.ifft(Fa*np.conj(Fw)).real
grad=2.0*r[(-np.arange(n))%L]
return grad
```
Need maybe L power of two. For n=512, M+n-1=1534, L=2048; fine. For n=4096, L=16384.
Check correctness with small example: a=[a0,a1], w=[w0,w1,w2]. L>=4. r[t]=∑ a_p w_{p-t}. grad0=2*r[L-0]=2*r[0]=2a1? Wait c_0=∑ a_p w_{p+0}=a0w0+a1w1. r[0]=∑ a_p w_{p}=a0w0+a1w1. Good. grad1=2*r[L-1]=2*r[-1]=∑ a_p w_{p+1}=a0w1+a1w2. For b0 weight w0, grad=[2a0? w0: grad0=2a0, grad1=0? Formula w only w0=1: r[0]=a0, r[-1]=a0*w1? w1=0 =>0. Good. For b1 w1=1: grad0=2a1, grad1=2a0. Correct. Good.

`def _rfftfting?` no.

`def _softmax_weights(b,n,tau):`
```
M=len(b); Bn=n*b; Bn -= Bn.max(); w=np.exp(tau*Bn); w/=w.sum(); return w
```
If Bn huge? b normalized. Max Bn ~J/2 ~1.5. tau up to 50, exp(-tau*diff) stable since subtract max.

`def _project_simplex_grad(a, g, step):`
```
a_new = a - step*g
a_new = np.maximum(a_new,0)
S=a_new.sum()
if S<=0: return a
return a_new/S
```

def _gradient_search(a0, deadline, max_iter=20000, step0=0.03, eval_every=1):

    n=len(a); best=a.copy(); bestJ=_score(a)

    mode_cycle length 4

       b=_conv_full(a); J=2n*b.max() (sum=1)

       else: stail+=1

          continue? but b stale? no matter
       mode=it%4
       if mode==0: p=b.argmax(); g=_grad_peak(a,p)

       elif mode==2: w=_top_weights(b,64); g=_grad_weighted(a,w)
       else: w=_softmax_weights(b,tau=20+10*((it//100)%3)); g=_grad_weighted(a,w)
       g -= g.mean(); gn=norm(g)+eps; g/=gn

       # accept minimally? We set a=a_new; next score decides. but update best not compare yet.

Band objective with basis:
```
def _band_objective(p, basis, n):
    r = float(p[0])
    if not np.isfinite(r): return 1e9
    if r < 0: r = -r
    if r > 1.0: r = 1.0
    if p[1:].size:
       y = basis.dot(p[1:])
    else: y = np.zeros(n)
    y -= y.mean()
    m = np.max(np.abs(y))
    if m < 1e-12:
       a = np.ones(n)
    else:
       a = 1.0 + r*(y/m)
       a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    S = a.sum()
    if S < 1e-12: return 1e9
    a /= S
    b = _conv_full(a)
    return float(2.0 * n * b.max())
```
Since sum1, no divide by S^2? Wait if a normalized sum1, score=2n max_b. Yes. Good.
`_band_from_p` similar returns normalized a.

Potential: If p[1:] huge, basis dot huge; y/m normalized. So amplitudes only direction; scale irrelevant due to maxabs. This means Nelder-Mead has scale-free amplitudes; r controls depth. Good. But if all amplitudes zero, uniform.

Random freqs: Could use deterministic ones: for n, bases = [ [0.25,0.75,1.25], [0.33,0.67,1.0,1.5], ...]. To avoid no effect, also sinusoidal phase via basis sin.

Maybe low-dimensional band optimization can escape full local minima. But full gradient from band start will refine. Good.

LP refine details with normalized a sum1:
```
def _lp_refine(a0, deadline, max_iters=200, min_factor=0.95):
    a=_normalize(a0); n=len(a); best=a; bestJ=_score_norm(a)
    # if n > 700 maybe only if time and max_iters small? Could handle up to 1024 with sparse if deadline enough
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time>deadline: break
       # ensure small positive for LP: f = a + 1e-6; renormalize
       f = a + 1e-6
       f /= f.sum()
       M = bestJ/(2.0*n)  # current peak for normalized a (conv sum1)
       # solve with M_current maybe, and if fail try M*1.01
       for fac in [1.0,1.01,1.02,0.98,0.95]:
           g=_lp_direction(f, M*fac)
           if g is None: continue
           g_norm = g/g.sum() if sum>0
           # try blends
           improved=False
           for t in [1.0,0.8,0.6,0.4,0.2,0.1,0.05]:
                cand = _normalize((1.0-t)*a + t*g_norm)
                J = _score_norm(cand)
                if J < bestJ - 1e-12:
                    a = cand; bestJ=J; best=a.copy(); improved=True; break
           if improved: break
       else:
           # no improvement from any fac; break or small perturbation
           perturb = _normalize(a + 0.01*np.random.default_rng(1234+it).normal(size=n))
           J=_score_norm(perturb)
           if J < bestJ: a=perturb; bestJ=J; best=a.copy()
           else: break
       print maybe every 20
    return best,bestJ
```

def _spectral_candidates(a_full, n, L, a_current, bestJ):
    cands=[]
    # shift candidates: 0, maybe best window of positive mass, center, around argmax a_full
    shifts = [0]
    # if L>n, compute sliding sum of positive values
    pos = np.maximum(a_full,0)
    if L > n:
       csum = np.zeros(L+1); csum[1:]=np.cumsum(pos)
       win = csum[n:L+1] - csum[:L+1-n]
       best_s = int(np.argmax(win))
       shifts.extend([best_s, max(0,best_s-n//4), min(L-n,best_s+n//4), L-n])
       # Also maybe around argmax of positive a_full
       amax_s = int(np.argmax(pos))
       s2 = max(0, min(L-n, amax_s - n//2)); shifts.append(s2)
    else: shifts=[0]
    unique shifts clipped 0..L-n.
    for s in unique:
       v=a_full[s:s+n]
       # clip
       c1=np.maximum(v,0); S1=c1.sum(); if >0 c1/=S1; append
       # shift-min
       mn=v.min()
       if mn < 0:
          c2=v - mn; S2=c2.sum(); if >0 c2/=S2; append
       # maybe if many zeros, add small eps?
       # Evaluate score and update local best
    return best_cand
```

def _optimize_band_one(n, freqs, rng, maxiter=120):
   basis=_build_band_basis(n,freqs)
   p=np.zeros(2*len(freqs)+1)
   p[0]=0.7
   p[1:]=rng.normal(0,0.5,len(p)-1)
   cur_sc=_band_objective(p,basis,n)
   cur_p=p.copy()
   try:
      res=minimize(_band_objective, p, args=(basis,n), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':maxiter,'xatol':1e-6,'fatol':1e-8,'adaptive':True})
      if res is not None and res.fun < cur_sc: cur_sc=float(res.fun); cur_p=res.x
   except Exception: pass
   seq=_band_seq_from_p(cur_p,basis)
   return seq, _score_norm(seq)
```

     ...

        # small perturbation

        if J<bestJ: a=a2; bestJ=J; best=a.copy()
        else: break
  if it%20==0: print
return best,bestJ
```

Let's detail each.

`_fast_len(m)`:
```
if m <=1: return 1
return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)*target
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.shape, target / max(1,a.size))
    return a * (target / S)
```
Clipping to 1000 may alter if input huge but okay.

`_conv_full`:
```
def _conv_full(a):
    n=a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([0.0])
    M=2*n-1
    if n < 128:
        b=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
        L=_fast_len(M)
        A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
        b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)
        b=b[:M]
    # clip tiny negatives
    maxneg = -1e-12 * max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(b))))
    if b.size and b.min() < maxneg:
        b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)
    return b
```
Using np.where could set all tiny negatives to 0; okay. But if actual negative due to FFT large error? Conv nonnegative. Use `b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)` maybe. But if a has values causing large b, tiny negative relative. Just `b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)`.

`_score_norm(a)`:
```
b=_conv_full(a); return float(2.0*a.size*max(0.0,b.max()))
```
Since normalized sum1. If a not normalized, we should use `_score_seq`. But we will pass normalized. To be safe:
```
def _score_norm(a):
   n=a.size; S=a.sum(); if S<=...: return inf; b=...; return float(2*n*b.max()/(S*S))
```
Use general `_score_seq` for any. But repeated S sum small. We'll implement `_score_seq(a)` and use for normalized. It will sum every time. Fine.
```
def _score_seq(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); S=a.sum(); n=len(a)
   if S<1e-12: return inf
   b=_conv_full(a); return float(2*n*b.max()/(S*S))
```
For normalized, same.

`_grad_peak` as above. For p maybe numpy int. Use int.

`_grad_top_direct`:
```
def _grad_top_direct(a, peaks, K):
   n=len(a); grad=np.zeros(n)
   for p in peaks: grad += _grad_peak(a,int(p))
   if K>1: grad /= K
   return grad
```
But if K duplicates? peaks distinct. Good.

`_grad_weighted`: as above. Need if w sum not 1? We'll normalize in weight functions. If all zero, return zeros.
```
def _grad_weighted(a,w):
   n=a.size; if n==0 return zeros
   M=2*n-1
   # w may length M; if longer, truncate; if shorter pad
   if w.size < M: w=np.pad(w, (0,M-w.size))
   else: w=w[:M]
   L=_fast_len(M+n-1)
   Fa=np.fft.fft(a[:n], L)
   Fw=np.fft.fft(w, L)
   r=np.fft.ifft(Fa*np.conj(Fw)).real
   idx = (-np.arange(n)) % L
   return 2.0*r[idx]
```
This picks r[-j]. Good.

`_top_weights(b,K)`:
```
def _top_weights(b,K):
   M=b.size; w=np.zeros(M)
   if K<=0: w[b.argmax()]=1; return w
   if K>=M: idx=np.arange(M); vals=b
   else:
      idx=np.argpartition(b, M-K)[M-K:]  # top K unsorted
      # if M-K ==0? K=M handled
   vals=b[idx]
   # weights maybe proportional to vals-min+1
   vmin=vals.min(); weights=vals-vmin
   if weights.sum() <=0: weights=np.ones_like(vals)
   weights/=weights.sum()
   w[idx]=weights
   return w
```
If K small and vals all equal, equal weights. Good. For K=1, argpartition works but use argmax.

`_softmax_weights(b,n,tau)`:
```
Bn = b * n
Bn = Bn - Bn.max()
w = np.exp(tau*Bn)
s=w.sum()
if s<=0: w[...] =1/M; 
else w/=s
return w
```
Since Bn max 0, exp stable. If tau=0, uniform.

`_project_neg(a,g,step)`:
```
a_new = a - step*g
a_new = np.where(a_new > 0, a_new, 0)
S=a_new.sum()
if S<=1e-15: return a.copy()
return a_new/S
```

`_gradient_search(a0, deadline, max_iter=100000, step0=0.03, seed=1)`:
Need handle if a0 score inf. Return.
```
def _gradient_search(a0, deadline, max_iter=100000, step0=0.03, seed=123):
    a=_normalize(a0)
    best=a.copy(); bestJ=_score_seq(a)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=a.size
    step=step0
    lastJ=None
    stale=0
    # precompute? no
    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
        b=_conv_full(a)
        J=float(2.0*n*b.max()) # sum approx 1
        if J < bestJ:
            bestJ=J; best=a.copy(); stale=0
        else:
            stale += 1
        if lastJ is not None:
            if J < lastJ * 0.999:
                step = min(0.25, step*1.05)
            elif J > lastJ * 1.02:
                step = max(1e-4, step*0.75)
            else:
                step = max(1e-4, step*0.97)
        if stale > 5000:
            a = _normalize(a + 0.03*rng.normal(size=n))
            step=step0
            stale=0
            continue
        mode = it % 4
        if mode == 0:
            p = int(np.argmax(b))
            g = _grad_peak(a,p)
        elif mode == 1:
            K = 8
            if K >= b.size:
                peaks=np.arange(b.size)
            else:
                peaks=np.argpartition(b, b.size-K)[b.size-K:]
            g = _grad_top_direct(a,peaks,K)
        elif mode == 2:
            w = _top_weights(b, 64)
            g = _grad_weighted(a,w)
        else:
            tau = 20.0 + 10.0*((it // 200) % 4)
            w = _softmax_weights(b,n,tau)
            g = _grad_weighted(a,w)
        g -= g.mean()
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gn < 1e-14: break
        g /= gn
        a = _project_neg(a,g,step)
        lastJ = J
    return best, bestJ
```
Note: J uses sum=1 assumption; after `_project_neg` normalized sum1. Initial a sum1. Good. If a is not normalized due to something, maybe error small. Could use `_score_seq` but slower sum. We can ensure all functions normalize. In LP perturb, normalize. Good.
`stale` counts solid 5000; maybe 1000. Use 2000 to escape sooner. For each mode, could get stuck. Use 3000.
Time: each iter maybe 0.005; max_iter 100000 enough.

`_paired_search(a0, deadline, ...)`:
```
def _paired_search(a0, deadline, max_iter=100000, step0=0.02, seed=321):
    a0=_normalize(a0); n=a0.size
    if n % 2 != 0 or n < 4:
       return a0, _score_seq(a0)
    m=n//2
    rev = a0[n-1:m-1:-1]  # length m
    pair_sums = a0[:m] + rev
    C=float(pair_sums.mean())
    if C <= 1e-15: C=2.0/m
    y = a0[:m]/C
    y = np.clip(y,0.0,1.0)
    # build function inline:
    def? avoid. We'll make a local variable and each iteration:
    a = np.empty(n)
    a[:m] = C*y
    a[n-1:m-1:-1] = C*(1.0-y)
    bestJ=_score_seq(a); best=a.copy()
    rng=...
    step=step0; lastJ=None; stale=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time>deadline: break
       b=_conv_full(a); S=C*m; J=2*n*b.max()/(S*S)
       if J<bestJ: update; stale=0 else stale++
       adapt step using lastJ
       if stale>3000: y=np.clip(y+0.05*rng.normal(size=m),0,1); build; step=step0; stale=0; continue
       mode... compute g_a using b
       g_y = g_a[:m] - g_a[n-1:m-1:-1]
       g_y -= g_y.mean(); gn=norm; if small break
       y = np.clip(y - step*(g_y/gn),0,1)
       a = np.empty(n); a[:m]=C*y; a[n-1:m-1:-1]=C*(1-y)
       lastJ=J
    return best(best), bestJ
```
Need ensure rev slice after a assignment works: `a[n-1 : m-1 : -1]` with start n-1, stop m-1 (exclusive), step -1. For n=6,m=3: start5 stop2 -> indices5,4,3 length3. Good. For n=4,m=2: start3 stop1 ->3,2 length2. Good.

`spectral_search` details:
```
def _spectral_search(a0, deadline, target_score=1.5, max_iter=10000, seed=55):
   a=_normalize(a0); n=a.size; M=2*n-1; L=_fast_len(M)
   best=a.copy(); bestJ=_score_seq(a)
   peak=b.max() ... T=peak
   T_end=target_score/(2*n)
   if T_end > T: T_end=T
   rng=...
   for it in range(max_iter):
      if time>deadline: break
      b=_conv_full(a); J=2*n*b.max()
      if J<bestJ: best...
      # T anneal
      T = max(T_end, 0.995*T)
      # target
      beta = min(0.65, 0.25*math.sqrt((min(it,500))/100.0)) # simplified
      target = (1-beta)*b + beta*(1.0/M)
      s=target.sum(); if s>0 target/=s
      # cap and fill
      target = np.minimum(target, T)
      s=target.sum()
      if s < 1.0:
          room = T - target
          tot=room.sum()
          if tot > 0:
             target += (1.0-s) * room / tot
          else:
             target = np.full_like(b, T)
             target[:int(1.0/T)] = ...? But T*M>1.
      else:
          target /= s
      F=np.fft.fft(target, L)
      F[0]=complex(max(0.0,F[0].real),0)
      A=np.empty(L, dtype=complex)
      half=L//2
      A[0]=complex(math.sqrt(max(0.0,F[0].real)),0)
      for k in range(1, half):
          ck=np.sqrt(F[k])
          A[k]=ck; A[L-k]=np.conj(ck)
      if L % 2 == 0:
          # Nyquist
          val=F[half].real
          A[half]=complex(math.sqrt(max(0.0,val)),0.0)
      a_full=np.fft.ifft(A).real
      # candidate shifts
      shifts=[0]
      if L > n:
          pos=np.maximum(a_full,0.0)
          csum=np.empty(L+1); csum[0]=0.0; csum[1:]=np.cumsum(pos)
          win = csum[n:L+1] - csum[:L+1-n]
          bs = int(np.argmax(win))
          shifts.extend([bs, max(0, bs-n//4), min(L-n, bs+n//4), L-n])
          am = int(np.argmax(pos))
          shifts.append(max(0, min(L-n, am - n//2)))
      # unique manual
      seen=[]
      for s in shifts:
         s=int(s)
         if s<0: s=0
         if s>L-n: s=L-n
         if s not in seen: seen.append(s)
      best_a_for_iter=None; best_iter_J=J
      for s in seen:
          v=a_full[s:s+n]
          c1=np.maximum(v,0.0); S1=c1.sum()
          if S1>1e-12:
             c1/=S1; J1=2*n*_conv_full(c1).max()
             if J1<best_iter_J: best_iter_J=J1; best_a_for_iter=c1
          mn=v.min()
          if mn < 0.0:
             c2=v-mn; S2=c2.sum()
             if S2>1e-12:
                c2/=S2; J2=2*n*_conv_full(c2).max()
                if J2<best_iter_J: ...
      if best_a_for_iter is not None:
          # accept if not too bad
          if J1_or_iter < J * 1.10 or best_iter_J < bestJ:
              a = best_a_for_iter.copy()
          else:
              # blend with current
              cand = _normalize(0.5*a + 0.5*best_a_for_iter)
              Jb=2*n*_conv_full(cand).max()
              if Jb < J: a=cand
      else:
          # keep a, maybe perturb if stale
          if bestJ - J > 1e-12? no
      # if many no move, perturb
      # T maybe not updating? If a unchanged, still T 0.995; target lower.
      if bestJ <= target_score: maybe break? We can continue to polish but not necessary. If bestJ < target_score: step T closer? We'll break after some? Could break to save time.
   return best,bestJ
```

Now `_lp_direction`:
```
def _lp_direction(f, M, deadline=None):
    n=f.size
    if n < 2: return f
    # ensure f positive
    f=np.asarray(f,float); f=np.maximum(f,1e-12); f/=f.sum()
    offsets=[0]
    diags=[np.full(n, f[0])]
    for d in range(1,n):
       vals=np.full(n-d, f[d])
       offsets.append(d); diags.append(vals)
       offsets.append(-d); diags.append(vals)
    A=sp.diags(diags, offsets, shape=(2*n-1,n), format='csc')
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M)
    bounds=[(0.0, 1000.0)]*n
    options={'disp': False}
    if deadline is not None:
       tl=int(max(3, deadline - time.monotonic()) )
       if tl>0: options['time_limit']=tl
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=options)
       if res.status == 0:
          g=np.asarray(res.x,float)
          g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
          if g.sum()>1e-12: return g
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
If f has tiny 1e-12, row constraints with d? Bound maybe large but ub1000. Good.
If time limit reached, res.status? Could be 12, no x. Return None. But if it uses time limit and still finds feasible? status maybe 1? We'll accept only 0.
Maybe `time_limit` not accepted by scipy's linprog; if unknown, options dict may raise `ValueError`? I think scipy checks known options? It might pass to solver ignoring? To be safe, don't set time_limit. Use max_iters small. LP solve for 512 likely fast. But could hang? We can set max_iters? HiGHS options `dual_edge_iter_limit`? Not. Maybe `time_limit` is supported. I checked scipy linprog highs options include `time_limit` (float) in scipy 1.9? Yes. Good.

`_lp_refine`:
Need use rng. Since f normalized sum1, a after g normalized sum1. Score compute `_score_seq` or `2n max`. To be safe, use `_score_seq` for blend. For current J, use `_score_seq` each loop or previous. Because after LP/f normalization sum maybe1. Use `_score_seq` small.
```
def _lp_refine(a0, deadline, max_iters=10000, seed=777):
    a=_normalize(a0); n=a.size; best=a.copy(); bestJ=_score_seq(a)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       J=_score_seq(a)
       if J<bestJ: best...; bestJ=J
       M=J/(2.0*n)
       f=a + 1e-7
       f/=f.sum()
       improved=False
       for fac in [1.0, 1.02, 0.98]:
           g=_lp_direction(f, M*fac, deadline)
           if g is None: continue
           gsum=g.sum(); if gsum<=0 continue
           g/=gsum
           for t in [1.0, 0.8, 0.5, 0.3, 0.15, 0.05]:
              cand=_normalize((1.0-t)*a + t*g)
              Jc=_score_seq(cand)
              if Jc < bestJ:
                  a=cand; bestJ=Jc; best=a.copy(); improved=True; break
           if improved: break
       if not improved:
           # try increasing M
           g=_lp_direction(f, M*1.05, deadline)
           if g is not None:
              g/=g.sum()
              cand=_normalize(0.5*a + 0.5*g); Jc=_score_seq(cand)
              if Jc < bestJ: ... improved=True
       if not improved:
           # perturb and maybe continue once
           a=new=_normalize(a + 0.02*rng.normal(size=n)); Jp=_score_seq(a)
           if Jp < bestJ: ...
           else:
              # Try t=1 with same g? already
              break
       if it % 20 ==0: print LP iter score
    return best,bestJ
```
This calls `_score_seq` multiple times per blend; expensive but LP dominates. For t blend, compute b each. Try 6 t; okay.
Could reduce t list [1.0,0.5,0.2]. Use [1.0,0.6,0.3,0.1]. Good.

Band search: Use `minimize` with `maxiter`. To avoid long, set maxiter 80. It returns object. We can call for multiple tries. Need time check before each minimize. If time left <30 break.
```
def _band_search(n, deadline, tries=3, seed=999):
   rng=default_rng(seed); best=None; bestsc=inf
   L=3
   for t in range(tries):
      if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20: break
      freqs = rng.uniform(0.2, 3.5, L)
      # ensure separated? no
      seq,sc=_optimize_band_one(n, freqs, rng, maxiter=80)
      if seq is not None and sc<bestsc: update
   return best,bestsc
```
`_optimize_band_one` if minimize fails returns initial. It computes score with `_score_seq`.

Potential issue: `scipy.optimize.minimize` with Nelder-Mead and args basis/n. `_band_objective` may be called many times; if p contains nan, return large.
```
def _band_objective(p,basis,n):
   if p.size ==0: return 1e9
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)): return 1e9
   r=float(p[0]); r=abs(r); if r>1: r=1
   if p.size>1:
      y=basis.dot(p[1:])
   else: y=np.zeros(n)
   y = np.asarray(y,float)
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)): return 1e9
   y -= y.mean()
   amax=np.max(np.abs(y))
   if amax<1e-12:
       a=np.ones(n)
   else:
       a=1.0 + r*(y/amax)
       a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   S=a.sum()
   if S<1e-12: return 1e9
   a = a / S
   b=_conv_full(a)
   return float(2.0*n*b.max())
```

No closures. Good.

for sc,idx,seq in items:
   if len(seen_n) >=3: break
   ...
```
Since items sorted, good. New results appended but not in loop. After loop, `items.sort()`.

- In len_best update, use helper inline:
```
if sc < best_score:
   best_score = sc; best_seq = seq.copy()
key = len(seq)
prev = len_best.get(key)
if prev is None or sc < prev[0]: len_best[key] = (sc, seq.copy())
```
Use repeated.

- Call `_lp_refine` with `deadline` can be far; it stops itself. But if we want allocate slice, use `dd = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 300.0)` and pass `dd`.

- In `_lp_refine`, inside loop it checks `time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0`. If deadline slice small, perhaps no iterations. Good.

- In final, if time left, run `_lp_refine(best_seq, deadline, max_iters=100000)` if len<=600. This may run until deadline, okay.

- If best length >600 and time left, run gradient/spectral. Use `_spectral_search` maybe expensive; if time > 90, use spectral; else gradient. Good.

Let's include final prints.

Now, let's ensure `_lp_refine` with no improvement breaks; it might break after first no improvement even if small perturbation could help. It tries perturbation and breaks if no improve. Good enough.

Potential improvement: In LP, after an improvement, continue. If it improves many times, good. If it stagnates, break. Could re-enter LP in final stage after gradient. Good.

One more mathematical consideration: Since we normalize sum to 1, LP constraints M ~ J/(2n). For n=512,J=1.5,M~0.00146. f plus floor min 1e-12; but f values ~0.002. Bounds g <=1000 (sum could be up to 512000) but constraints limit. Good.

Now, final code. I will include enough comments but not excessive. Ensure final only code fence.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for a low-peak non-negative self-convolution sequence.

Strategy:
- Keep the best known candidate globally, plus best per length.
- Use multiple complementary optimizers:
  1. LP refinement (max sum(g) s.t. f*g <= current peak) for small lengths.
  2. Paired/complement local search (a_i plus mirrored coordinate sum fixed).
  3. Full projected gradient / subgradient search with top-peak and softmax weights.
  4. Spectral square-root projection toward a flatter target convolution.
- Start from the previously discovered sequence if available, plus low-cost
  analytic/random Fourier starts and band-limited starts optimized by Nelder-Mead.
- Time is sliced so that the function always returns its best sequence.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog
import scipy.sparse as sp


def _fast_len(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([target], dtype=np.float64)
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.shape, target / max(1, a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (target / S)


def _conv_full(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    if n < 128:
        b = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        L = _fast_len(M)
        A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)
        b = b[:M]
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)


def _score_seq(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    S = float(a.sum())
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or S <= 1e-15:
        return float('inf')
    b = _conv_full(a)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(b)) / (S * S))


def _score_norm(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    S = float(a.sum())
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or S <= 1e-15:
        return float('inf')
    b = _conv_full(a)
    return float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(b)) / (S * S))


def _grad_peak(a, p):
    n = int(a.size)
    p = int(p)
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lo = max(0, p - n + 1)
    hi = min(n - 1, p)
    if hi >= lo:
        idx = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        g[idx] = 2.0 * a[p - idx]
    return g


def _grad_top_direct(a, peaks, K):
    g = np.zeros_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(K)
    if k <= 0:
        return g
    for p in peaks:
        g += _grad_peak(a, int(p))
    if k > 1:
        g /= float(k)
    return g


def _grad_weighted(a, w):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    if w.size < M:
        w = np.pad(w, (0, M - w.size), mode='constant')
    else:
        w = w[:M]
    L = _fast_len(M + n - 1)
    Fa = np.fft.fft(a, L)
    Fw = np.fft.fft(w, L)
    r = np.fft.ifft(Fa * np.conj(Fw)).real
    idx = (-np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)) % L
    return 2.0 * r[idx]


def _top_weights(b, K):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    M = int(b.size)
    w = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
    K = int(K)
    if K <= 0:
        w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return w
    if K >= M:
        idx = np.arange(M)
        vals = b
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(b, M - K)[M - K:]
        vals = b[idx]
    vmin = float(np.min(vals))
    weights = vals - vmin
    s = float(np.sum(weights))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        weights = np.ones(idx.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(idx.size)
    weights /= s
    w[idx] = weights
    return w


def _softmax_weights(b, n, tau):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    M = int(b.size)
    Bn = b * float(n)
    Bn -= float(np.max(Bn))
    if tau <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(M, 1.0 / M, dtype=np.float64)
        return w
    w = np.exp(float(tau) * Bn)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.full(M, 1.0 / M, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def _project_nonneg(a, g, step):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    a_new = a - step * g
    a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0)
    S = float(a_new.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
        return a.copy()
    return a_new / S


def _gradient_search(a0, deadline, max_iter=100000, step0=0.03, seed=123):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a, float('inf')
    best = a.copy()
    bestJ = _score_norm(a)
    if not np.isfinite(bestJ):
        return best, bestJ
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    step = float(step0)
    lastJ = None
    stale = 0

    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        b = _conv_full(a)
        J = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(b)))
        if J < bestJ:
            bestJ = J
            best = a.copy()
            stale = 0
        else:
            stale += 1

        if lastJ is not None:
            if J < lastJ * 0.999:
                step = min(0.25, step * 1.05)
            elif J > lastJ * 1.02:
                step = max(1e-4, step * 0.75)
            else:
                step = max(1e-4, step * 0.97)

        if stale > 1200:
            a = _normalize(a + 0.03 * rng.normal(size=n))
            step = float(step0)
            stale = 0
            continue

        mode = it % 4
        if mode == 0:
            g = _grad_peak(a, int(np.argmax(b)))
        elif mode == 1:
            K = 8
            if K >= b.size:
                peaks = np.arange(b.size)
            else:
                peaks = np.argpartition(b, b.size - K)[b.size - K:]
            g = _grad_top_direct(a, peaks, peaks.size)
        elif mode == 2:
            w = _top_weights(b, 64)
            g = _grad_weighted(a, w)
        else:
            tau = 20.0 + 10.0 * float((it // 200) % 4)
            w = _softmax_weights(b, n, tau)
            g = _grad_weighted(a, w)

        g -= float(np.mean(g))
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(g))
        if gn < 1e-14:
            break
        g /= gn

        a = _project_nonneg(a, g, step)
        lastJ = J

    return best, bestJ


def _paired_search(a0, deadline, max_iter=100000, step0=0.02, seed=321):
    a0 = _normalize(a0)
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n == 0 or (n % 2) != 0:
        return a0, _score_norm(a0)
    m = n // 2
    rev = a0[n - 1:m - 1:-1]
    pair_sums = a0[:m] + rev
    C = float(np.mean(pair_sums))
    if C <= 1e-15:
        C = 2.0 / float(m)
    y = np.clip(a0[:m] / C, 0.0, 1.0)

    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:m] = C * y
    a[n - 1:m - 1:-1] = C * (1.0 - y)

    best = a.copy()
    S = C * float(m)
    bestJ = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(_conv_full(a))) / (S * S))
    if not np.isfinite(bestJ):
        return best, bestJ

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    step = float(step0)
    lastJ = None
    stale = 0

    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        b = _conv_full(a)
        J = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(b)) / (S * S))
        if J < bestJ:
            bestJ = J
            best = a.copy()
            stale = 0
        else:
            stale += 1

        if lastJ is not None:
            if J < lastJ * 0.999:
                step = min(0.25, step * 1.05)
            elif J > lastJ * 1.02:
                step = max(1e-4, step * 0.75)
            else:
                step = max(1e-4, step * 0.97)

        if stale > 1200:
            y = np.clip(y + 0.05 * rng.normal(size=m), 0.0, 1.0)
            a[:m] = C * y
            a[n - 1:m - 1:-1] = C * (1.0 - y)
            step = float(step0)
            stale = 0
            continue

        mode = it % 4
        if mode == 0:
            g_a = _grad_peak(a, int(np.argmax(b)))
        elif mode == 1:
            K = 8
            if K >= b.size:
                peaks = np.arange(b.size)
            else:
                peaks = np.argpartition(b, b.size - K)[b.size - K:]
            g_a = _grad_top_direct(a, peaks, peaks.size)
        elif mode == 2:
            w = _top_weights(b, 64)
            g_a = _grad_weighted(a, w)
        else:
            tau = 20.0 + 10.0 * float((it // 200) % 4)
            w = _softmax_weights(b, n, tau)
            g_a = _grad_weighted(a, w)

        g_y = g_a[:m] - g_a[n - 1:m - 1:-1]
        g_y -= float(np.mean(g_y))
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(g_y))
        if gn < 1e-14:
            break
        g_y /= gn

        y = np.clip(y - step * g_y, 0.0, 1.0)
        a[:m] = C * y
        a[n - 1:m - 1:-1] = C * (1.0 - y)
        lastJ = J

    return best, bestJ


def _make_spectral_target(b, T, n, it):
    M = int(b.size)
    flat = 1.0 / float(M)
    beta = min(0.60, 0.30 * math.sqrt(min(float(it), 400.0) / 100.0))
    target = (1.0 - beta) * b + beta * flat
    s = float(np.sum(target))
    if s > 0.0:
        target = target / s
    else:
        target = np.full(M, flat, dtype=np.float64)

    target = np.minimum(target, T)
    s = float(np.sum(target))
    if s < 1.0:
        room = T - target
        tot = float(np.sum(room))
        if tot > 1e-15:
            target += (1.0 - s) * room / tot
        else:
            target = np.full(M, max(T, flat), dtype=np.float64)
            target /= float(np.sum(target))
    elif s > 1.0:
        target /= s
    return target


def _spectral_search(a0, deadline, target_score=1.495, max_iter=100000, seed=555):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a, float('inf')
    best = a.copy()
    bestJ = _score_norm(a)
    if not np.isfinite(bestJ):
        return best, bestJ
    if n < 2:
        return best, bestJ

    M = 2 * n - 1
    L = _fast_len(M)
    T = float(np.max(_conv_full(a)))
    T_end = float(target_score) / (2.0 * float(n))
    if T_end > T:
        T_end = T
    if T_end < 1e-12:
        T_end = 1e-12

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    stale = 0
    it = 0

    while it < max_iter:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        b = _conv_full(a)
        J = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(b)))
        if J < bestJ:
            bestJ = J
            best = a.copy()
            stale = 0
        else:
            stale += 1

        if stale > 150:
            a = _normalize(a + 0.02 * rng.normal(size=n))
            b = _conv_full(a)
            J = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(b)))
            if J < bestJ:
                bestJ = J
                best = a.copy()
            T = max(T_end, J / (2.0 * n))
            stale = 0
            it += 1
            continue

        T = max(T_end, 0.995 * T)
        target = _make_spectral_target(b, T, n, it)

        F = np.fft.fft(target, L)
        A = np.empty(L, dtype=np.complex128)
        A[0] = complex(math.sqrt(max(0.0, float(F[0].real))), 0.0)
        half = L // 2
        if L % 2 == 0:
            if half > 1:
                A[1:half] = np.sqrt(F[1:half])
                A[half + 1:] = np.conj(A[1:half][::-1])
            if half < L:
                A[half] = complex(math.sqrt(max(0.0, float(F[half].real))), 0.0)
        else:
            if half > 0:
                A[1:half + 1] = np.sqrt(F[1:half + 1])
                A[half + 1:] = np.conj(A[1:half + 1][::-1])

        a_full = np.fft.ifft(A).real

        pos = np.maximum(a_full, 0.0)
        if L >= n:
            csum = np.zeros(L + 1, dtype=np.float64)
            csum[1:] = np.cumsum(pos)
            win = csum[n:L + 1] - csum[:L + 1 - n]
            best_s = int(np.argmax(win))
            if best_s > L - n:
                best_s = L - n
            if best_s < 0:
                best_s = 0
        else:
            best_s = 0

        shifts = [best_s, 0]
        uniq_shifts = []
        for s in shifts:
            s = int(s)
            if s < 0:
                s = 0
            if s > L - n:
                s = L - n
            if s not in uniq_shifts:
                uniq_shifts.append(s)

        chosen = None
        chosenJ = None
        for s in uniq_shifts:
            v = a_full[s:s + n]
            c1 = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
            S1 = float(c1.sum())
            if S1 > 1e-12:
                c1 /= S1
                J1 = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(_conv_full(c1))))
                if chosenJ is None or J1 < chosenJ:
                    chosenJ = J1
                    chosen = c1

            mn = float(np.min(v))
            if mn < 0.0:
                c2 = v - mn
                S2 = float(c2.sum())
                if S2 > 1e-12:
                    c2 /= S2
                    J2 = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(_conv_full(c2))))
                    if chosenJ is None or J2 < chosenJ:
                        chosenJ = J2
                        chosen = c2

        if chosen is not None and chosenJ is not None:
            if chosenJ < bestJ or chosenJ < J * 1.05:
                a = chosen.copy()
        else:
            a = _normalize(a + 0.02 * rng.normal(size=n))

        it += 1

    return best, bestJ


def _lp_direction(f, M, deadline=None):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(f.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return f.copy()
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-12)
    S = float(f.sum())
    if S <= 0.0:
        return None
    f = f / S

    diags = []
    offsets = []
    for i in range(n):
        diags.append(np.full(n, float(f[i]), dtype=np.float64))
        offsets.append(i)

    try:
        A_ub = sp.diags(diags, offsets, shape=(2 * n - 1, n), format='csc')
    except Exception:
        return None

    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * n
    options = {'disp': False}
    if deadline is not None:
        tl = int(max(2, deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if tl > 0:
            options['time_limit'] = tl

    try:
        res = linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=options)
        if res.status == 0 and res.x is not None:
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
            if g.sum() > 1e-12:
                return g
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def _lp_refine(a0, deadline, max_iters=100000, seed=777):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a, float('inf')
    best = a.copy()
    bestJ = _score_norm(a)
    if not np.isfinite(bestJ):
        return best, bestJ
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    it = 0
    while it < max_iters:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break

        J = _score_norm(a)
        if J < bestJ:
            bestJ = J
            best = a.copy()

        M = J / (2.0 * float(n))
        f = a + 1e-9
        f = _normalize(f)

        improved = False
        for fac in (1.02, 1.0, 0.98):
            g = _lp_direction(f, M * fac, deadline)
            if g is None:
                continue
            gs = float(g.sum())
            if gs <= 1e-12:
                continue
            g = g / gs

            for t in (1.0, 0.6, 0.3, 0.1):
                cand = _normalize((1.0 - t) * a + t * g)
                Jc = _score_norm(cand)
                if Jc < bestJ:
                    a = cand
                    best = cand.copy()
                    bestJ = Jc
                    improved = True
                    break
            if improved:
                break

        if not improved:
            g = _lp_direction(f, M * 1.05, deadline)
            if g is not None:
                gs = float(g.sum())
                if gs > 1e-12:
                    g = g / gs
                    cand = _normalize(0.5 * a + 0.5 * g)
                    Jc = _score_norm(cand)
                    if Jc < bestJ:
                        a = cand
                        best = cand.copy()
                        bestJ = Jc
                        improved = True

        if not improved:
            cand = _normalize(a + 0.02 * rng.normal(size=n))
            Jc = _score_norm(cand)
            if Jc < bestJ:
                a = cand
                best = cand.copy()
                bestJ = Jc
            else:
                break

        if it % 20 == 0:
            print(f"  LP iter {it}: best={bestJ:.6f}")
        it += 1

    return best, bestJ


def _build_band_basis(n, freqs):
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)[:, None]
    parts = []
    for fr in freqs:
        ang = 2.0 * math.pi * float(fr) * idx
        parts.append(np.cos(ang))
        parts.append(np.sin(ang))
    if len(parts) == 0:
        return np.zeros((n, 0), dtype=np.float64)
    return np.concatenate(parts, axis=1)


def _band_seq_from_p(p, basis):
    n = basis.shape[0]
    if p.size == 0:
        return _normalize(np.ones(n))
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return _normalize(np.ones(n))
    r = abs(float(p[0]))
    if r > 1.0:
        r = 1.0
    if p.size > 1:
        y = basis.dot(np.asarray(p[1:], dtype=np.float64))
    else:
        y = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)):
        return _normalize(np.ones(n))
    y -= float(np.mean(y))
    amax = float(np.max(np.abs(y)))
    if amax < 1e-12:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = 1.0 + r * (y / amax)
        a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def _band_objective(p, basis, n):
    a = _band_seq_from_p(p, basis)
    if a.size == 0:
        return 1e100
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-15:
        return 1e100
    b = _conv_full(a)
    return float(2.0 * int(n) * float(np.max(b)) / (S * S))


def _optimize_band_one(n, freqs, rng, maxiter=80):
    basis = _build_band_basis(n, freqs)
    p0 = np.zeros(basis.shape[1] + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    p0[0] = 0.7
    if p0.size > 1:
        p0[1:] = rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, size=p0.size - 1)
    best_p = p0.copy()
    best_sc = _band_objective(p0, basis, n)
    try:
        res = minimize(_band_objective, p0, args=(basis, n), method='Nelder-Mead',
                       options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'xatol': 1e-5,
                                'fatol': 1e-8, 'adaptive': True})
        if res is not None:
            try:
                if float(res.fun) < best_sc:
                    best_sc = float(res.fun)
                    best_p = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            except Exception:
                pass
    except Exception:
        pass
    seq = _band_seq_from_p(best_p, basis)
    sc = _score_norm(seq)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        seq = _normalize(np.ones(n))
        sc = _score_norm(seq)
    return seq, sc


def _band_search(n, deadline, tries=2, seed=999):
    best = None
    best_sc = float('inf')
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    for t in range(int(tries)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0:
            break
        L = 3 + (t % 2)
        freqs = rng.uniform(0.2, 3.5, size=L)
        seq, sc = _optimize_band_one(n, freqs, rng, maxiter=70 if n > 1024 else 100)
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = seq.copy()
        print(f"  band search n={n}, attempt {t}: score={best_sc:.6f}")
    return best, best_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    if 'budget_s' in kwargs:
        try:
            budget = float(kwargs['budget_s'])
        except Exception:
            pass
    budget = max(120.0, min(2400.0, budget))
    deadline = t0 + max(90.0, budget - 75.0)

    target_score = 1.495

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float('inf')
    len_best = {}
    items = []
    item_id = 0

    def add_candidate_local(seq, score_override=None):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score, item_id
        seq = _normalize(seq)
        if seq is None or seq.size == 0:
            return
        if score_override is None:
            sc = _score_norm(seq)
        else:
            sc = float(score_override)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            return
        seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()
        k = int(seq.size)
        prev = len_best.get(k)
        if prev is None or sc < prev[0]:
            len_best[k] = (sc, seq.copy())
        items.append((sc, item_id, seq.copy()))
        item_id += 1

    # Previous construction, if available.
    try:
        h = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if h.size >= 4:
            if h.size <= 4096:
                add_candidate_local(h)
            else:
                for L in (512, 1024, 2048, 4096):
                    if h.size >= L:
                        add_candidate_local(h[:L])
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Basic uniform starts.
    for n in (512, 1024, 2048):
        add_candidate_local(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # A few very cheap analytic/Fourier starts.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0:
        for n in (512, 1024):
            i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
            denom = max(1, n - 1)
            a1 = _normalize(1.0 + 0.8 * np.cos(math.pi * i / denom))
            a2 = _normalize(1.0 + 0.6 * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * 0.73 * i))
            a3 = _normalize(np.sin(math.pi * i / denom) ** 2 + 0.1)
            a4 = _normalize(1.0 + 0.4 * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * 1.31 * i)
                               + 0.3 * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * 0.37 * i))
            for a in (a1, a2, a3, a4):
                add_candidate_local(a)

    # Band-limited starts.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 700.0:
        seqb, scb = _band_search(512, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 120.0), tries=2, seed=11)
        if seqb is not None:
            add_candidate_local(seqb, scb)
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 500.0:
        seqb, scb = _band_search(1024, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 100.0), tries=2, seed=22)
        if seqb is not None:
            add_candidate_local(seqb, scb)

    # Spectral/short-gradient pass on best few distinct lengths.
    items.sort()
    seen_lengths = set()
    for sc, idx, seq in items:
        if len(seen_lengths) >= 3:
            break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 420.0:
            break
        k = int(seq.size)
        if k in seen_lengths:
            continue
        if k > 4096:
            continue
        seen_lengths.add(k)

        alloc = 130.0 if k <= 1024 else 80.0
        dd = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + alloc)
        seq2, sc2 = _spectral_search(seq, dd, target_score=target_score,
                                     max_iter=100000, seed=1000 + k)
        if seq2 is not None:
            add_candidate_local(seq2, sc2)

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 80.0:
            seq3, sc3 = _gradient_search(seq2, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 35.0),
                                         max_iter=20000, step0=0.03, seed=2000 + k)
            if seq3 is not None:
                add_candidate_local(seq3, sc3)

    items.sort()

    # Intensive polishing for short sequences, preferring 512 because LP is effective.
    for k in (512, 256, 1024):
        if k not in len_best:
            continue
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 350.0:
            break
        sc_prev, seq_prev = len_best[k]
        print(f"  Refining length {k}, before={sc_prev:.6f}, time_left={deadline - time.monotonic():.1f}s")

        if k <= 600 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 220.0:
            seq_lp, sc_lp = _lp_refine(seq_prev, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 260.0),
                                       max_iters=100000, seed=3000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_lp, sc_lp)

        if (k % 2) == 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 180.0:
            seq_pair, sc_pair = _paired_search(len_best.get(k, (sc_prev, seq_prev))[1],
                                               min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 140.0),
                                               max_iter=100000, step0=0.02,
                                               seed=4000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_pair, sc_pair)

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 150.0:
            seq_grad, sc_grad = _gradient_search(len_best.get(k, (sc_prev, seq_prev))[1],
                                                 min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 160.0),
                                                 max_iter=50000, step0=0.03,
                                                 seed=5000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_grad, sc_grad)

        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0:
            seq_spec, sc_spec = _spectral_search(len_best.get(k, (sc_prev, seq_prev))[1],
                                                 min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 110.0),
                                                 target_score=target_score,
                                                 max_iter=30000, seed=6000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_spec, sc_spec)

    # Focus on the current global best for the remaining useful time.
    if best_seq is not None:
        k = int(best_seq.size)
        if k <= 600 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
            print(f"  Final LP polish length {k}, before={best_score:.6f}")
            seq_lp, sc_lp = _lp_refine(best_seq, deadline, max_iters=200000, seed=7000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_lp, sc_lp)
        elif time.monotonic() < deadline - 70.0:
            print(f"  Final spectral polish length {k}, before={best_score:.6f}")
            seq_spec, sc_spec = _spectral_search(best_seq, deadline,
                                                 target_score=min(1.495, best_score * 0.995)
                                                 if np.isfinite(best_score) else 1.495,
                                                 max_iter=200000, seed=8000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_spec, sc_spec)
        elif time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
            seq_grad, sc_grad = _gradient_search(best_seq, deadline, max_iter=300000,
                                                 step0=0.02, seed=9000 + k)
            add_candidate_local(seq_grad, sc_grad)

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64)
        best_score = _score_norm(best_seq)

    best_seq = _normalize(best_seq)
    final_score = _score_norm(best_seq)
    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        best_seq = _normalize(np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64))
        final_score = _score_norm(best_seq)

    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_seq]
    print(f"Final internal score: n={len(final_list)}, score={final_score:.6f}")

    try:
        ev = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        print(f"evaluate_sequence final: {ev}")
        if not np.isfinite(ev) and np.isfinite(final_score):
            # If external evaluator unexpectedly rejects or overflows, fall back.
            pass
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"evaluator unavailable/error: {e}")

    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
        print("Deadline reached; returning immediately.")

    return final_list
```
